Important information about compensation arrangements The account(s) listed in this statement are eligible for cover under the Financial Services Compensation Scheme (FSCS). We're covered by the FSCS. The FSCS compensate depositors if a bank can't meet its financial obligations. Most depositors – including most individuals and businesses – are covered by the scheme. We'll give you the FSCS information sheet and exclusions list once a year. This tells you what is and isn't covered by the FSCS in lots of detail. The FSCS protects eligible deposits up to £85,000 per person. The FSCS apply this limit to each person, and to the total combined amount of any money you have with the provider. For joint accounts, each account holder has a claim to their share separately. That means that in a joint account held by two eligible depositors, the maximum amount each person could claim is £85,000 each (for a total of £170,000). For more information about FSCS compensation, head to the FSCS website at www.FSCS.org.uk Monzo Bank Limited (https://monzo.com) is a company registered in England No. 9446231. - PDF The Insolvency Group: Full and Final - Information Regarding Early Settlement of Your IVA [10732/7] From: Hafiz Jawad (The Insolvency Group) | info@theinsolvencygroup.hubsolv.com To: joe.joerey@gmail.com Attachments: Third-Party-FF-Letter.docx 21 October 2025 at 10:37 You are receiving this email because you are currently subscribed to receive emails from The Insolvency Group. Dear Joe, Thank you for your recent communication regarding the possibility of an early settlement of your IVA. Below, we have outlined the key details and steps involved in settling your IVA early, along with the required documentation and considerations to ensure the process goes as smoothly as possible. First thing first, you should not draw down the pension until acceptance from Creditors. This demonstrates that the funds won't be made available to the Creditors unless they accept to settle. How to Settle Your IVA Early An IVA can be settled early by paying a single lump sum into the arrangement instead of making the remaining monthly payments. This offer must be formally proposed to your creditors by us. 1. 2. Discuss the Offer Amount: The first step is to discuss the amount you can offer. If we believe the offer is likely to be accepted, we will present it to your creditors in a variation meeting. Creditor Approval: For the offer to be accepted, a voting outcome of 75% of the voting creditors must be obtained. This is the same requirement as when the IVA was originally agreed. How Much Will You Need to Pay? The lump sum does not need to match the total outstanding debt but must represent a reasonable proportion of the remaining balance. Creditors often view early settlements favourably because they receive payment sooner than expected. If your circumstances have not changed, the offer will need to be close to the total of your remaining payments. See ‘Summary of Contributions’ for further details. Creditors may accept a slightly lower amount, but offering much less is unlikely to succeed unless your circumstances have changed significantly, and the IVA is at risk of failure. Important: The funds used for settlement must usually come as a gift from a third party. If you have recently received a windfall (e.g., inheritance, compensation, or a lottery win), this money must be paid into your IVA as part of the agreement and cannot be used for settlement purposes. Steps to Proceed with a Settlement If you wish to proceed with a settlement, we will require the following: 1. Documentation of Your Financial Situation: Last three months' payslips (if employed) or your latest self-assessment tax return (if self- employed). Proof of all household benefits being received (even if this is a sole IVA). Last three months' bank statements for all accounts you hold. A full Universal Credit breakdown, if applicable. 1. A Letter of Instruction: A written explanation for the offer, including reasons for the proposed settlement (e.g., changes in financial circumstances). 1. Third Party Offer Details: A letter from the third party providing the funds (template attached if required - This must be signed by the third party), including: Full name, date of birth, and contact details of the third party. Proof of ID and address for the third party. Evidence of funds available (must include third party name on document and dated within the last three months). Signed consent for an anti-money laundering check. Important: Do not send any settlement funds until your offer is formally approved by creditors. If you do, the monies will be distributed to creditors, and if your offer is rejected, it will be very difficult to recover the funds. Summary of Contributions We can provide a settlement figure based on funds gifted by a third party. However, creditors are unlikely to approve offers that are significantly lower than what was originally anticipated unless there are extenuating circumstances. The contributions originally agreed upon in your IVA are as follows: Voluntary Contributions: £269 (equivalent to 72 monthly contributions of £19368) To date, you have contributed the following amounts into your IVA: Voluntary Contributions: £269 Please note that if your affordability has improved following a review of your income and expenditure, the offer will need to reflect the future contributions creditors would expect to receive. This means the above summary could change. We recommend making the highest possible offer to maximise the chances of creditor approval. Next Steps If you wish to proceed, please contact us to confirm and submit the required documentation. Upon receipt of your request and all required documentation, a member of the team will review these within 14 working days. A proposal will then be prepared to present to creditors. Once this has been reviewed and approved by a member of our compliance team, it will be sent to you for review and approval. Upon receiving your consent, the report will be circulated to creditors within 3 working days. Important Timelines: Creditors must be given a minimum of 28 days to consider the proposal, with an additional allowance for delivery and receipt. This means a 31-day notice period will apply for the meeting. On the day of the meeting, a member of our team will call you to confirm the outcome. There are three possible outcomes: Adjournment (no longer than 14 days). Approval. Rejection (in which case, your IVA would continue under its original terms). Should you have any questions or require further clarification, please do not hesitate to contact us on 0161 543 2310. We are here to support you through this process and ensure the best possible outcome for your IVA. Right to cancel You have the right to cancel your order for Sky Broadband without giving any reason any time up to 14 days ("cooling off period") from the later of: (i) delivery of your Wireless Router; (ii) activation; or (iii) receipt of the relevant terms and conditions for that product/service. Sky may offer an enhanced cooling off period from time to time which we will advise you of at the time of your purchase. If you take fibre-based Sky Broadband and you request an engineer appointment, a separate cancellation policy applies to installation services which is contained in the Sky Fibre Broadband Installation Terms and Conditions. If you cancel a contract during your cooling off period we will refund to you all payments received from you, including the costs of delivery of any equipment you ordered e.g. your Wireless Router ("Equipment"), but if you requested a service to begin during the cooling off period, you must pay us an amount which is proportionate to the service provided up to the point you cancelled your order, including any chargeable Sky Talk calls you have made. You will not receive a refund for any one-off fees for activation or set up services if you cancel a service after activation. We will make any refund due to you (less any deductions due to us) without undue delay and not later than the earliest of 14 days after the day we either receive the Equipment back from you or receive evidence from you that the Equipment has been returned (such as a proof of posting receipt). We will make the refund using the same means of payment that you used when you placed your order, unless you have agreed otherwise. You may end this contract during your minimum term without paying early termination charges if we make a change which is not exclusively to your benefit unless it is purely administrative in nature or directly imposed by law and we have notified you of that right. You can cancel your Sky order by: (i) calling 03442 41 44 14; (ii) writing to Sky Subscribers Services Limited, PO Box 43, Livingston, West Lothian EH54 7DD; or (iii) by visiting the "Contact Us" section at sky.com and completing the online cancellation form. Sky may offer an enhanced cooling off period from time to time which we will advise you of at the time of your purchase If you cancel a contract during your cooling off period we will refund to you all payments received from you, including the costs of delivery of any equipment you ordered e.g. your Wireless Router ("Equipment"), but if you requested a service to begin during the cooling off period, you must pay us an amount which is proportionate to the service provided up to the point you cancelled your order, including any chargeable Sky Talk calls you have made. You will not receive a refund for any one-off fees for activation or set up services if you cancel a service after activation. We will make any refund due to you (less any deductions due to us) without undue delay and not later than the earliest of 14 days after the day we either receive the Equipment back from you or receive evidence from you that the Equipment has been returned (such as a proof of posting receipt). We will make the refund using the same means of payment that you used when you placed your order, unless you have agreed otherwise You may end this contract during your minimum term without paying early termination charges if we make a change which is not exclusively to your benefit unless it is purely administrative in nature or directly imposed by law and we have notified you of that right. You can cancel your Sky order by: (i) calling 03442 41 44 14; (ii) writing to Sky Subscribers Services Limited, PO Box 43, Livingston, West Lothian EH54 7DD; or (iii) by visiting the "Contact Us" section at sky.com and completing the online cancellation form. If you decide to cancel or switch either your Sky Talk or your Sky Broadband services to another provider, both will be cancelled at the same time unless we tell you otherwise. You will not be able to re-order Sky Broadband without Sky Talk. Data Protection In line with GDPR, we updated our Privacy and Cookies Notice to clearly explain the data we collect, how we use it, and what rights and tools are available to you. We update the notice every so often, so it's best to keep checking back to see how we use your data, you can access the notice here www.sky.com/help/articles/gdpr Your rights to how we collect and handle your data include: • to request access to, or erasure of, the personal data we hold about you (we explain more about this below). • to request us to restrict the processing of the personal data we hold about you. • to object to us processing personal data relating to you. • where you have given us consent to process your personal data, you have the right to withdraw that consent at any time. • you have the right to obtain certain personal data from us in a format that can be transferred electronically to a third party (also called "data portability"). Please note that some of these rights are not absolute. In some cases, for example, we may refuse a request to exercise particular rights if complying with it meant that we are no longer able to meet our contractual obligation to provide you with particular products and services, or if we had a legal obligation or right not to comply with the request. However, we will keep you informed as to the actions that we can take, when you make your request. Accessibility and Vulnerable Customers We want to make sure you receive the most from your Sky services. We provide specific services to our customers with accessibility needs, such as: • Electronic, large print or braille bills for our visually impaired customers. • Free Directory Information and Directory Enquiry Facilities. • Access to a Relay Service for those who need to make or take calls in text format. • Access to emergency services, Operator Assistance and a Directory Enquiry service using short code numbers. • A priority fault repair service for registered accessibility customers, who have a genuine need for an urgent repair. • The ability to work with an authorised, registered nominee or Power of Attorney, who can manage the customer's account on their behalf. • Emergency SMS (eSMS) for Sky Mobile • eSMS is a third-party service you can register for at any time, and an add-on to the current 999 and 18000 services in the UK.
